## Document 028: 2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは5年に1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が67％を占めていた（資料1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、 「安全運用が基本」とした（注1） 。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。 「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注3）。GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、 「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、 「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、 「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである4資産を25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、 「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注11） 。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に3％台に達し、約30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、 「国内債券の利回りが3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注12）。 
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注13）。2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

## Document 043: 20261013_投資週報_標普指數獲利預期上修，科技股仍為主要動能

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261013_投資週報_標普指數獲利預期上修，科技股仍為主要動能.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 1

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

資料中心監管疑慮衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅 ；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。
表一：美股主要指數表現（2026/11/05 收盤）
主要指數 收盤點位 單日漲跌幅
S&P 500 指數 7,353.73 −4.36%
Nasdaq 綜合指數 24,315.00 −5.52%
費城半導體指數 9,011.14 −12.25%
資料來源：Reuters、Nasdaq、元大銀行整理
2. 波動率指數急升，公債殖利率全面走低，油價反彈：11 月 5 日避險情緒升溫，VIX 盤
中升至 28.09（前日 16.61）。資金湧入美債，2 年、5 年、10 年及 30 年期殖利率分
別降至 4.46%、4.59%、4.84%及 5.25%，呈牛市陡峭化；12 月升息一碼機率由約
83%降至約 65%。布蘭特原油反彈 3.88%至每桶 98.29 美元，通膨前景仍具不確定性
（詳見表二） 。

二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨橫掃國會及州長選舉，資料中心開發成為州級政策議題：德州州長當選人 Gina
Hinojosa 主張暫停新建資料中心，俄亥俄州與喬治亞州當選人也提出限制。地方政府

可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，
半導體、電力設備及資料中心類股成為賣壓重心。
2. 高通手機晶片業績低於預期，加重半導體類股賣壓：高通財年第四季調整後 EPS 年減
28%至 2.17 美元，手機晶片營收年減 26%至 51.37 億美元，低於預期，主因蘋果相
關收入減少；汽車與物聯網營收雖年增 36%，仍難抵銷缺口，股價隔日收跌 9.5%，
進一步放大半導體跌幅。
3. 融資成本與投資回收壓力加重，依賴外部融資業者首當其衝：資料中心資本支出多發

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正 ， 評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。
表三：11/5 主要科技股及類股表現
標的 單日漲跌幅 重點說明
費城半導體指數 −12.25% 選後跌幅最深，AI 供應鏈評價重估
高通（Qualcomm） −9.5% 手機晶片營收年減 26%，低於預期
軟體類股 ETF（IGV） −4.48% 跌幅明顯小於半導體
資料來源：Reuters、Bloomberg、元大銀行整理

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、 營業利益年增209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。
表四：AI 基本面持續擴張──關鍵指標一覽
指標 最新數據 解讀
Google Cloud 第三季營收 年增 103% AI 需求快速轉化為收入
Google Cloud 第三季營業利益 年增 209% 獲利能力未因擴張而惡化
四大雲端業者資本支出預估 約 8,732 億→1.16 兆美元 企業主動加碼，投資循環仍擴張
資料來源：元大投顧、Bloomberg；元大銀行整理

不另行通知。 
2. 留意五大反彈訊號，利空逐步消化後市場可望回穩：（一）州級電價與併網制度若於
2027 年上半年 建立 ，延後 專案可望 下半年重新加速 ，眾議院 以 417 比 3 通過
《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。 
表五：S&P 500 第三季財報統計（截至 2026/11/04） 
類別 已公布家數 優於預期家數 優於預期比率 第三季 EPS 成長率 
S&P 500 397 317 79.85% 29.0%

7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢 ， 切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

## Document 106: 2026年三季度货币政策执行报告学习体会：货币政策趋稳

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

2026 年三季度货币政策执
行报告学习体会

货币政策趋稳   
2026 年三季度货币政策执行报告内容改动之处不多，我们认为，一定程度上意
味着货币政策中长期目标“建设金融强国”成为未来一段时间内的主要任务。 
 2026 年三季度货币政策执行报告中，相比二季度执行报告改动的内容不多： 
 一是对世界经济的判断，从“动能疲弱”变为“韧性不足”，以及出现了“主要经济
体经济表现分化” ， 对于通胀和货币政策 ， 二季度中提到的“全球通胀水平有所抬升，
主要经济体央行货币政策趋向调整”，也调整成了三季度的“通胀走势和货币政策调
整存在不确定性” 。 我们认为主要原因在于两方面 ， 一是2026 年带动全球经济增长的
人工智能科技发展，随着美国中期选举结果落定，开始遭受监管压力，二是推升2026
年通胀的国际能源价格，也有可能随着美国对外战略的调整，结束供给紧张的格局。
但是从全球范围内来看，一方面通胀有效回落需要时间，另一方面经济增长动力切换
不太容易，因此不排除在特定时间内，全球经济增长韧性面临“通胀处于高位、经济
增速下行、货币政策收紧”的三重考验。 
 二是在国内经济形势判断的部分，二季度的判断是“经济长期向好的内在支撑更加坚
实”，三季度则提出“仍面临供强需弱、结构分化、外部冲击等问题和挑战”。我们
认为这一提法对应的是我国经济增长表现出的两个特点，一是从内部来看，与全球经
济增长类似的 K 型分化，以电子行业为代表的人工智能产业链，对我国进出口、工业
增加值、工业企业利润数据都形成较明显的拉动，二是从外部影响看，2026 年前三个
季度三驾马车中净出口的累计同比贡献率逐步上升。因此无论是从产业链角度，或全
球化角度，我国经济都更容易受全球经济增长的影响。 
 三是关于增量政策和政策协同，二季度货币政策执行报告中提到的是“充分发挥各项
存量政策效能 ， 及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策” ， “加强与财政政策协同配合”，
“发挥好货币政策工具总量和结构双重功能”，三季度则是“发挥存量政策与增量政
策的集成效应”和“发挥好各类结构性货币政策工具作用”，三季度货币政策执行报
告中，增加了“‘六张网’建设”作为金融支持的重点领域之一。我们认为该部分内
容的调整，主要指向的是货币政策的“效能”而非一直以来货币政策宽松指向的“增
量”，可能较大程度上会降低市场对于货币政策持续宽松的预期。 
 四是货币政策改革更加具体，二季度货币政策执行报告中提到“推动贷款定价基准多
元化”，在三季度货币政策执行报告中已经具体为“推动贷款利率定价基准从比较单
一地参考 LPR 定价向更加多元的定价基准转型” ， 并且“目前已有参考 DR001 定价
的贷款陆续落地”。我们认为现阶段货币政策改革的目标不仅是降低实体经济融资成
本，同时也是加强货币政策传导的改革。 
 五是专栏的部分依旧是五个 ， 我们比较关注的是“专栏 3 设立债市非银大额赎回互换
便利”。“专栏 2 国际利率水平比较”也有提到，我国当前利率、利差，无论是与历
史水平相比还是与海外相比，都处于偏低水平，因此货币政策工具创设和使用的一个
重要考虑，都是“为经济稳定增长、高质量发展和金融市场平稳运行营造适宜的货币
金融环境”。 
 我们认为下一阶段货币政策的重要任务是聚焦完成中长期目标，也就是“金融强国”
建设。 
 三季度我国货币政策大事包括 ：7 月 24 日 ， 中国人民银行联合科技部等8 部门印发 《关
于加强科技金融领域数据开发利用的通知》 ；8 月 14 日，中国人民银行、国家外汇管
理局联合发布《关于跨国公司本外币跨境资金集中运营业务有关事宜的通知》；8 月
28 日，中国人民银行会同金融监管总局印发《关于改革完善房地产信贷管理 推动加
快构建房地产发展新模式的意见》；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行联合金融监管总局、中
国证监会、国家发展改革委、工业和信息化部、财政部、商务部、文化和旅游部印发
《关于金融支持服务业扩能提质的指导意见》 ；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行印发《关于
抵押补充贷款政策加力支持“六张网”建设有关事宜的通知》，下调抵押补充贷款利率
0.25 个百分点至 1.50%，将“六张网”建设纳入抵押补充贷款支持领域；9 月 28 日，中
国人民银行办公厅印发《关于加大科技创新和技术改造再贷款政策支持力度有关事宜
的通知》 ，增加科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度2000 亿元，并将该项再贷款支持比例
从 60%提高至 100%；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行决定增加支农支小再贷款额度 5000
亿元 ， 其中民营企业再贷款额度增加 3000 亿元 ， 激励引导地方法人金融机构扩大对涉
农、小微和民营企业的信贷投放；9 月 29 日，财政部会同中国人民银行、金融监管总
局印发《关于实施居民购房贷款贴息政策的通知》。此外，还有同部分国家续签双边
本币互换协议，以及任命海外人民币业务清算行事宜。 
 我们认为下一阶段货币政策可能采取对内“稳”对外“进”的方针：对国内经济和金融市
场以稳定为主，重点在于营造适宜经济高质量发展和平稳运行的货币金融环境，货币
政策方面，增量政策工具可能减少，政策重点在于发挥存量政策效能和加大现有货币
政策应用；对国外以推进人民币国际化、打造“人民币-人民币资产”循环为主，保持人
民币汇率弹性，不断增强人民币和人民币资产对国际资本的吸引力。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；全球经济增长动能减弱；地缘政治局势发展前景的不
确定性。

当前外部环境更加复杂多变， 世界经济增长韧性不足， 地缘冲突
和经贸摩擦多发频发，主要经济体经济表现分化，通胀走势和
货币政策调整存在不确定性。我国经济主要指标积极变化增多，
高质量发展取得新成效，但仍面临供强需弱、结构分化、外部
冲击等问题和挑战，既要坚定信心，保持高质量发展战略定力，
也要切实巩固拓展经济稳中向好态势。 
当前外部环境更加复杂多变，世界经济增长动能疲弱，地缘冲
突和经贸摩擦多发频发，供给冲击和输入型通胀压力影响仍在
持续，全球通胀水平有所抬升，主要经济体央行货币政策趋向
调整。我国经济高质量发展取得新成效，后劲持续增强，经济
长期向好的内在支撑更加坚实，制度优势和大国优势不断彰显，
要坚定信心，保持高质量发展战略定力，切实巩固拓展经济稳
中向好态势。 
坚持稳中求进工作总基调， 完整准确全面贯彻新发展理念，发挥
存量政策与增量政策的集成效应，加大逆周期调节力度，加力
扩大内需、 优化供给， 推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，努力完
成全年经济社会发展目标任务。坚定不移走中国特色金融发展
之路， 进一步深化金融改革和高水平对外开放， 加快建设金融强
国， 完善中央银行制度， 构建科学稳健的货币政策体系和覆盖全
面的宏观审慎管理体系，畅通货币政策传导机制。

发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策 ，
加大逆周期调节力度，加力扩大内需、优化供给，推动经济持

一步深化金融改革和高水平对外开放， 加快建设金融强国， 完善
中央银行制度， 构建科学稳健的货币政策体系和覆盖全面的宏观
审慎管理体系，畅通货币政策传导机制。

作 
坚持实施好适度宽松的货币政策，为经济稳定增长、高质量发
展和金融市场平稳运行营造适宜的货币金融环境。 继续实施好适度宽松的货币政策。 
增强政策前瞻性灵活性针对性， 根据国内外经济金融形势和金融
市场运行情况，综合运用并适时调整货币政策工具，把握好政
策实施的力度、 节奏和时机， 保持流动性充裕和社会融资条件相
对宽松， 引导社会融资规模、 货币供应量增长同经济增长、 价格
总水平预期目标相匹配。 
增强政策前瞻性灵活性针对性， 根据国内外经济金融形势和金融
市场运行情况， 把握好政策实施的力度、 节奏和时机，加强与财
政政策协同配合，支持经济稳定增长、高质量发展和金融市场
平稳运行。 
综合运用并适时调整货币政策工具，保持流动性充裕和社会融
资条件相对宽松， 引导社会融资规模、 货币供应量增长同经济增
长、价格总水平预期目标相匹配。 
稳妥有序推动货币政策操作框架改革完善， 更好引导货币市场短
端利率围绕政策利率平稳运行。发挥市场利率定价自律机制作
用，加强利率政策执行和监督，推动贷款利率定价基准从比较
单一地参考贷款市场报价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价
基准转型， 持续深化明示企业贷款综合融资成本工作， 降低融资
中间费用，促进社会综合融资成本低位运行。 
平稳有序推动货币政策操作框架改革完善，更好引导货币市场
短端利率围绕政策利率平稳运行。发挥市场利率定价自律机制
作用，加强利率政策执行和监督，推动贷款定价基准多元化，
引导金融机构提高利率定价能力，持续深化明示企业贷款综合融
资成本工作，降低融资中间费用， 促进社会综合融资成本低位运
行。

发挥好各类结构性货币政策工具作用，不断完善工具设计和管
理，扎实做好金融“五篇大文章”，加强对扩大内需、科技创新、
中小微企业、“六张网”建设等重点领域的金融支持。持续做好
支持民营经济发展壮大的金融服务。 
发挥好货币政策工具总量和结构双重功能，落实好年初出台的
一系列货币金融政策，不断完善工具设计和管理，扎实做好金
融“五篇大文章”， 加强对扩大内需、 科技创新、 中小微企业等重
点领域的金融支持。

坚持以市场供求为基础、 参考一篮子货币进行调节、 有管理的浮
动汇率制度， 保持汇率弹性， 发挥汇率调节宏观经济和国际收支
自动稳定器功能， 综合施策， 增强外汇市场韧性， 稳定市场预期，
防范汇率超调风险， 保持人民币汇率在合理均衡水平上的基本稳
定。 
坚持以市场供求为基础、 参考一篮子货币进行调节、 有管理的浮
动汇率制度， 保持汇率弹性， 发挥汇率调节宏观经济和国际收支
自动稳定器功能， 综合施策， 增强外汇市场韧性， 稳定市场预期，
防范汇率超调风险， 保持人民币汇率在合理均衡水平上的基本稳
定。

拓展丰富中央银行宏观审慎和金融稳定功能，完善工具箱， 维护
金融市场平稳运行，持之以恒做好风险防范化解工作，坚决守
住不发生系统性金融风险的底线。 
拓展丰富中央银行宏观审慎和金融稳定功能，创新金融工具，维
护金融市场平稳运行，坚决守住不发生系统性金融风险的底线。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数

Phison Electronics Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 gross margin miss; our
conservative stance on Nand
modules playing out
Looking back to our February downgrade note: In AI boosts NAND demand, but
Asian module maker margins may soon compress, we noted that Phison's gross
margin could decline in 2Q26 or 3Q26, once it ran out of low-cost inventory.  While
Phison's low-cost inventory cushioned the consumer Nand module margin downside,
its overall NAND module margin started to miss in 3Q26, which means the AI

storage part of its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue)
may see margin decline.
Please see Exhibit 1  for our earnings review. The company's 3Q26 gross margin
declined to 59%, below our 63.5% and the street's 60.3%, also missing the
company's previous commentary about a sustainable gross margin in 3Q26 given its
low cost inventory. The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business
can pick up and offset the margin pressure in the conventional module business in

the coming quarters.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
ChipMOS Technologies
Q326 earnings ahead on better GM; optics and
AI ASIC testing support 2027E growth
Strong margin recovery drives Q326 EPS beat
Q326 EPS came in ahead at NT$1.89 vs. UBSe/street of NT$1.27/1.47 due to higher
margins. Q326 sales was already posted through monthly sales at NT$8.4bn, +14%
QoQ,  supported  by robust  demand from memory (51% of sales, +13% QoQ) on
improving commodity DRAM volume and better momentum from driver IC due to OLED

iPhone ramp  (27% of DDIC sales, +46% QoQ), while it also raised memory backend ASP
to reflect rising component cost. Q326 GM improved to 21.7% (+3.7ppt QoQ) on better
pricing, lower pass through for bumping and higher loading, and  exceeded our estimate
of 18.6%. Q326 OPEX remains well controlled at 6.4% of sales, while it also booked
one-off other operating income of NT$373mn, resulting in better OPM of 19.7%.
Memory and OLED momentum to sustain into Q426

ChipMOS sees better momentum for memory over driver IC business into Q426F  on
continued strength from commodity DRAM and improving NAND Flash outlook. For its
DDIC/bumping business, despite softer end-demand for consumer electronics,
particularly PC and Android smartphone, ChipMOS noted the seasonal build for OLED
iPhone should provide some cushion for its driver IC sales. Management noted it will
continue to optimize its product portfolio and divest lower-end capacity, while

negotiates with its customers on pass through higher component cost if needed. We
forecast ChipMOS's Q426E sales to grow 2% QoQ, with GM expanding by 0.3ppt QoQ
to 22.0% on better  product mix and normalized electricity cost.
Optics and AI ASIC testing support 2027E growth
Management noted its copper bumping for US customers' next-generation products
remain under qualification and expects to see more meaningful revenue contribution by
late-2027F or 2028F. In the meantime, it is  working with the customer on producing

some legacy products in 2027F, although revenue contribution remains limited. As
optics-related business carries significantly better pricing than traditional DDIC
bumping, ChipMOS will prioritize its driver IC/bumping resource for optics and OLED
DDI, instead for Korean customer's outsourcing. Separately, ChipMOS continues to
expand its ASIC testing roadmap, having already entered mass production for
smartphone AP and expecting to progress to smartphone SoC testing, followed by ASIC

final test opportunities if the current program ramps successfully through H127.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy rating with price target of NT$155
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-23% on  improving GM profile driven by more favorable
mix but trim our PT from NT$166 to NT$155 as we apply a lower multiple of 20x 2027-
28E average PE (from 23x) given the recent market valuation contraction. Reiterate Buy
rating on improving margin profiles new AI (optics and logic testing) initiatives.

Company Description
ChipMOS is a semiconductor back-end supplier with 45-50% of its sales from display driver
IC (~40% from TV and 40% from smartphone and IT and 20% from automotive), 35-40%
from memory (~40% DRAM, ~40% from specialty flash and ~20% from mainstream NAND)
and 10-15% from logic (TV SoC) and mixed signal IC (MEMS, PMIC).

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target for ChipMOS is based on a target PE multiple. Downside risks include a
weaker-than-expected tech end-market demand recovery, growing China competition,
failure to diversify into the SiPh/optical component bumping business and a slower ramp of its
logic testing business.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12
months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA)  is the one-year local
market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged
by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

such as structure, management, performance record and discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management,
performance record and discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record and discount. Core

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

研 究  報 告                
啟碁(6285.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26 毛利率承壓 
 投資評價與建議 
受惠Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA滲透率提升且低軌衛星UT與800G交換器逐步放量，預
估2026/2027 年稅後EPS為13.25/14.23元，過往啟碁forward PE區間落在
15~20倍 ， 考量原物料成本上漲壓抑毛利率 ， 基於2027年獲利 ， 給予15倍PER 。 
 重點評論分析 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營
收季增23.5%至487億元，係因北美客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts
至12.2% ， 係因3Q26調整售價僅單純反映原物料漲價成本 ， 並非等比例調整售
價 ， 導致毛利率承壓 ， 目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準 ， 稅後EPS
為4.35元 。 預估4Q26營收季增3.1%至501.8億元 ， 由於目前原物料漲價品項多
且公司與客戶間多有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁
成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎：展望2026~2027年，主要營收動能來自Wi-Fi 
7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶終端裝置部署量持續提升。
啟碁目前交換器業務主要透過Tier 1 Switch供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，
下世代1.6T規格以及OCS預於2027年送樣 ，目標2028年開始貢獻營收。 4Q26
為是否於美國設廠之關鍵期，公司將視FCC、USMCA與關稅政策等以及客戶
需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管之產品線遷至美國生產。 
 投資風險 
原物料漲價與缺料、低軌衛星產品出現競爭者。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營收季增23.5%至487億元，主要受惠北美洲
Service Provider與Enterprise客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts至12.2%，係因3Q26調 整售價僅單純反映原物
料漲價成本，並非等比例調整售價，導致毛利率承壓，目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準，稅後EPS為4.35
元。隨Enterprise、Service Provider與Automotive三大事業群持續開拓新產品、專案與客戶，預估4Q26營收季增3.1%
至501.8億元，由於目前原物料漲價品項多，從Memory擴及PCB板、MLCC、成熟製程IC等零組件，且公司與客戶間多
有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，公司表示
4Q26至2027全年將持續受到缺料與漲價影響，達供需平衡仍需時間，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎，800G Switch與低軌衛星產品挹注營收動能：展望2026~2027年，啟碁主要營收動能來
自歐美Service Provider與Enterprise客戶之Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶 終端裝置部署量持
續提升。企業交換器業務方面，啟碁目前沒有計劃供應Data Center Switch予CSP，目前業務模式僅是透過Tier 1 Switch
供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，主要原因為公司自身之資源集中於Campus Switch以及Neo-Cloud市場，目前已取得
相當不錯之進展與成果，下世代1.6T規格Switch以及Optical Circuit Switch(OCS)預計於2027年開始送樣，目標於2028
年開始貢獻營收，公司預估Switch業務在未來2至3年內可實現70~90%之年成⻑率。產能方面，目前公司主要生產基地
位於越南廠，產能比重達約6成，近期公司表示2026年累計之資本支出達30億元，主要用於越南擴廠和美國建廠需求，
4Q26為美國設廠關鍵期，是否設廠將視FCC、USMCA、關稅政策等政策以及客戶需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管
之產品線遷至美國生產，初期階段先從SKD半散裝料開始，穩定後推進至CKD全散裝料生產，未來不排除將電信運營商
相關製造服務移至美國據點。綜上所述，預估2026/2027年營收年增51.9%/24.3%至1,674.7/2,082.1億元，稅後EPS為
13.25/14.23元。

## Document 259: [005380] 현대차(005380) 3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up현대차(005380) 3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up현대차(005380) 3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up3Q26 Review_ 4분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up - 기업.pdf

(005380)

이재일 CFA 
6183 / lee.jae-il@eugenefn.com

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율

## Document 300: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Key points
• The Cotality home value index – covering
all of Australia – fell 1.2% in October
following a 1.3% decline in September
and 1.4% falls in August and July.

Annual growth has turned negative,
running at –2.8%yr with prices down
6.7% from their peak in March.
• Across the major cities, Brisbane and
Perth led the declines in the October
month, recording falls of 2.0% and
1.7% respectively, but the correction
remains more pronounced in Sydney
(prices down a further 1.6%mth to be
off 9.4%yr) with Melbourne also off
more sharply over the year (–7.6%yr)
but recording a milder October
(–0.8%mth). Adelaide remains relatively

resilient, recording a 1.2% decline in
the month but prices still up 3.7%yr.
• Preliminary estimates show the
number of sales, in rolling 3mth
terms, down 5.6%qtr and 22.5%yr.

### Page 2

forecasts are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia, fell
1.2% in October with slight revisions to previous months
again shaving a further 0.3% off prices. The correction
has now seen prices decline 6.7% from their March peak

with annual growth firmly in negative at –2.8%yr. Turnover
continues fall as well, down 22.5%yr. Housing-related
tax policy changes, interest rate rises and heightened
uncertainty have triggered a sharp contraction in markets.
The downturn is now comparable to the previous two
housing market corrections. Nationally, the downturn in
2022-23 saw prices decline 7.5% and turnover fall 29% peak
to trough. The 2017-18 correction saw prices fall 8.6% and

turnover by 24%, with larger falls in Sydney and Melbourne.
Note that unless specified, figures in titles, tables and
commentary are in non‑seasonally adjusted terms, consistent
with the ‘headline’ figures reported by Cotality. However,
figures shown in charts are in seasonally adjusted terms.
Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 5.6%qtr in October, previous estimates again revised
lower. The monthly pattern suggests declines re ‑accelerated

through September‑October after stabilising somewhat in July ‑
August. The RBA's interest rate rise in late September and a
further expected tightening likely contributed to the shift.
The detail shows Brisbane and Perth are now leading the
way in terms of monthly price declines although corrections
to date remain significantly larger in Sydney and Melbourne
while smaller capitals and regional areas continue to see much
milder downturns. Across price tiers, all segments are now

showing similar rates of decline after top price tiers had seen
a more pronounced and earlier downturn. 'Top tier' segments
have tended to lead recent price cycles.
Looking across jurisdictions,
• Sydney prices dropped 1.6% in October having averaged
similar‑sized declines over the previous five months. While
all segments and tiers continue to record falls, the last few
months has seen the pace of decline moderate a touch in the
top tier segments that have led cycles in the past, although

they are still a long way from stabilisation.
• Melbourne prices declined 0.8%mth, in line with
September's move and continuing the slower pace of
retreat. As noted, this likely reflects the weaker starting
point coming into the correction, prices already at 5½ year
lows. Interestingly, the detail shows the moderation in price
declines in recent months has been more pronounced for
'top tier' units where prices are back where they were in
2016.
Housing downturn deepens

### Page 3

forecasts are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially
from these forecasts.
3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
• Brisbane prices are tracking a very fast rate of decline
that is also seeing large downward revisions. Prices fall
2% in October with September and August now both
showing 2.1% falls, revised down from 1.5% and 1.6%

respectively (August having previously been estimated at
just 1%, July's move having seen a similar series of revisions
–1.2% to –1.8%). The combined effect has taken the rolling
3mth change to –6.1%qtr from –4.7%qtr last months and
–2.7%qtr in August and –0.6%qtr in July. At 0.4%yr, annual
price growth has virtually stalled flat having run at 18%yr as
recently as May. All tiers and segments are recording similar
rates of decline.
• Perth is seeing something very similar, prices dropping a

further 1.7% in October, with September's decline revised
down to 2.1% from –1.2% and August's to 2.0% from –1.7%.
At 3.9%yr, annual growth is still positive but off steeply
from the 24% pace back in May. As with Brisbane, all tiers
and segments are recording similar rates of decline.
• Adelaide dwelling prices posted a somewhat milder 1.2%
decline in October, , September's –1.3% decline marked
down to –1.7%. Annual price growth slowed to 3.7%yr,

similar to Perth's but with less of a whiplash from strong
gains to large declines. Turnover has also been much
steadier, sales holding flat over the last three months and
down just 12.4%yr.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.6%mth, 9.7%yr), relatively resilient
in Hobart (+0.5%mth, 5.5%yr) but weaker in Canberra
(–0.7%mth, –3.7%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.6%). Annual

prices growth is in the 1.4 ‑3.5%yr range.
All up, the October updates make for bleak reading. With the
RBA widely expected to raise interest rates again, the near
term outlook remains very challenging. Next week's consumer
sentiment update will show how this is shaping housing ‑
related sentiment – so far, consumer price expectations have
weakened but stopped short of swinging to the clear negative
consensus that emerged in the 2022 ‑23 correction. That now

looks to be at risk. Either way, momentum looks set to remain
weak into year‑end and, barring a significantly improved
inflation and interest rate outlook, well into 2027.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

### Page 4

4

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

## Document 362: CoreWeave季报暂缓AI放缓担忧：营收暴增168%、全年指引上调，但债务依然高企｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave季报暂缓AI放缓担忧：营收暴增168%、全年指引上调，但债务依然高企｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CoreWeave 发布强劲季报，营收同⽐暴增 168% 、利润率双超预期，并上调全年收⼊及运
营利润指引，暂时缓解了市场对 AI 基础设施需求放缓的担忧，电话会后股价上涨约 5% 。但
财务结构压⼒依然突出：季末债务升⾄ 469 亿美元，单季新增逾百亿； Q4 利息⽀出预计⾼
达 11 亿美元，仍将超过同期运营利润。增⻓故事在继续，债务账单也在继续。
摘要：
AI 云计算提供商 CoreWeave 发布强劲季报，并上调全年收⼊指引，暂时缓解了市场对这家⾼负
债企业增⻓放缓的担忧。
第三季度， CoreWeave 营收 36.61 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 168% ，超出分析师预期的 35.6 亿美元，也
突破了公司⾃⾝指引上限 36 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 42% 。单季收⼊增量⾸次超过 10 亿美元。每股净
亏损 1.27 美元，好于市场预期的 1.46 美元。公司将2026 年全年收⼊指引上调⾄ 130 亿⾄ 133 亿美
元。
⾸席执⾏官 Michael Intrator 将这份季报定性为⼀个转折信号： " 市场叙事改变了，⼈们开始审视

AI 的建设进程。⽽我们的业绩表明，这家公司正在以正确的⽅式运转，并赢得了客⼾的持续选
择。 "
受业绩提振， CoreWeave 盘后股价⼀度⼤涨约 10% ⾄ 54 美元，但涨幅很快收窄，此后在电话会
议结束后⼜上涨约 5% 。该股今年以来累计跌幅仍约 32% ，较 2025 年 6 ⽉ 183 美元的⾼点已下跌逾
七成，此前六次财报中有五次在发布后下跌。
公司财务压⼒并未消散：季末债务单季暴增 118 亿美元⾄ 469 亿美元， Q4 利息⽀出预计⾼达 11 亿
美元，仍将超过同期调整后运营利润。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

营收与利润双超预期
CoreWeave 第三季度营收 36.61 亿美元，⾼于分析师预期的 35.6 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 168% 。每
股净亏损 1.27 美元，优于市场预期的 1.46 美元亏损。
调整后 EBITDA 达 22.56 亿美元，调整后 EBITDA 利润率为 62% ，与去年同期基本持平，⾼于市场
预期的 57.6%。调整后运营利润率升⾄ 10% ，较 Q2 的 5% 和 Q1 的 1% ⼤幅回升。⾸席执⾏官
Michael Intrator 在财报声明中表⽰，这⼀双位数利润率较原计划提前⼀个季度实现。

### Page 3

公司还披露，截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，收⼊积压订单约为 1325 亿美元，较 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇的 1042 亿美元环⽐增加
280 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 2.4 倍。 Intrator 表⽰，未来三个⽉内预计将收到约 90 亿美元的客⼾预付
款和押⾦，为公司运营提供充裕的流动性⽀撑。
全年指引上调，资本开⽀同步提升
CoreWeave 将 2026 年全年收⼊指引从此前的 124 亿⾄ 132 亿美元上调⾄130 亿⾄ 133 亿美元，这
意味着 Q4 营收约为 47 亿⾄ 50 亿美元，⽽华尔街此前预期集中在该区间下沿。 调整后运营利润指
引区间提升⾄ 12.75 亿⾄ 13.75 亿美元，中值较此前指引上涨约 26% ，隐含 Q4 调整后运营利润约
8 亿美元，超过本季度的两倍。
公司还将 2026 年末年化营收运⾏率⽬标从 185 亿⾄ 195 亿美元上调⾄ 200 亿⾄ 210 亿美元。
与此同时，公司上调全年资本开⽀指引下限，将区间从 350 亿⾄ 390 亿美元调整为 370 亿⾄ 390 亿

美元。管理层解释称，这反映了基础设施部署和交付客⼾的进度快于预期。
Q3 资本开⽀达 145 亿美元，不仅超出公司⾃⾝指引上限（ 115 亿⾄ 135 亿美元）逾 10 亿美元，更
是⼀年前 18.5 亿美元的近 8 倍。上半年已⽀出 162 亿美元，这意味着 Q4 仅剩 60 亿⾄ 80 亿美元的空
间，才能落在新的 370 亿⾄ 390 亿美元区间内 —— 资本开⽀提前部署的逻辑意味着，要么⽀出已
被前置，要么 390 亿美元的上限很快将再度被突破。
在基础设施层⾯， CoreWeave 本季度新增逾 200 兆⽡有效功率，总有效功率超过 1.7 吉⽡，年初
⾄今实现翻倍。已签约总功率超过 4.6 吉⽡，并将国际布局延伸⾄印度、印度尼西亚和西班⽛，
海外签约功率超过 1.3 吉⽡。
然⽽，⼀个值得关注的细节是：已签约功率中仅有 37% 处于激活状态，低于上季度的 41% 。年
末有效功率⽬标维持在 1.85 吉⽡以上不变，意味着 Q4 仅需新增约 150 兆⽡，是近⼀年来最慢的

### Page 4

增速 —— ⽽与此同时，资本开⽀却在提前部署。这⼀⽭盾或意味着 Q4 营收增⻓更多依赖价格⽽
⾮规模扩张。
债务账单： Q4 单季利息逼近 11 亿美元
尽管业绩数据令市场短暂松了⼀⼝⽓，结构性财务压⼒依然突出。第三季度净利息⽀出达 8.46 亿
美元，远⾼于 3.79 亿美元的调整后运营利润。
管理层在展望中明确披露：
Q4 利息中值 11 亿美元，较本季度的 8.46 亿美元⾼出 30% ，是⼀年前 3.88 亿美元的近三倍，年化
达 44 亿美元。以年末 200 亿⾄ 210 亿美元的营收运⾏率计算，每收⼊ 5 美元中就有超过 1 美元要直
接⽀付给债权⼈ —— 这还在折旧之前（ Q3 折旧达 18.9 亿美元，相当于营收的 52% ）。
即便按上调后的指引， Q4 调整后运营利润约 8 亿美元，利息⽀出仍将是运营利润的约 1.4 倍。这
虽较 Q3 的 2.2 倍和 Q1 匪夷所思的 25 倍有所改善，但资本结构依然处于 " 倒挂 " 状态。
D.A. Davidson 分析师 Gil Luria 在接受采访时直⾔： " 其利息⽀出远⾼于调整后运营利润 —— 这说

明数据中⼼产⽣的业务收益不⾜以偿还不断累积的债务。这是⼀个存在结构性挑战的商业模
式，这正是公司⻓期承压的原因。 "
CoreWeave 此前通过⼤规模融资补充流动性。本季度公司完成了约 42 亿美元的可转换优先票据
的超额发⾏，并设⽴了最多 3500 万股 A 类普通股的股票市价发⾏计划，同时完成了 26 亿美元的
DDTL 5.5 融资设施。
债务规模： 469 亿美元，每天新增 1.28 亿
CoreWeave 截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的资产负债表债务达 469 亿美元，较 6 ⽉底的 351 亿美元单季激增 118 亿
美元，较年初的 214 亿美元翻倍有余，较⼀年前的 140 亿美元增⻓逾两倍。折算下来， 92 天内⽇
均新增债务约 1.28 亿美元，包括周末。
我们预计 2026 年 Q4 利息⽀出区间为 10.5 亿⾄ 11.5 亿美元。

### Page 5

资产负债表其他关键项⽬：
10.6 亿美元的短期债务将在 12 个⽉内到期，⽽⾮受限现⾦仅 41 亿美元（另有 23 亿美元受限现
⾦）
经营租赁负债（不计⼊债务）从年初 82 亿美元飙升⾄ 187 亿美元，合并计算后固定债务义务
⾼达 656 亿美元
总负债 875 亿美元，对应股东权益仅 40 亿美元；债务权益⽐约为 1165% ，⽽ 6 ⽉底时 CNBC 援
引数据为 702%
为何债务增速持续超过营收？因为公司现⾦消耗惊⼈： Q3 经营性现⾦流为 19.8 亿美元，但⽤于
购置物业和设备的现⾦⽀出达 94.4 亿美元，净现⾦消耗 74.6 亿美元，创单季历史新⾼；前三季度
累计净消耗 179 亿美元， 2025 年初⾄今累计达 252 亿美元。资⾦缺⼝的填补⽅式⼀如既往：本季
度新增债务 103 亿美元，年初⾄今累计新增 270 亿美元。
融资困境：谁来买单 2027 年的 660 亿资本开⽀？
华尔街对 CoreWeave 融资缺⼝的测算差异悬殊： Bernstein 估计，仅为交付现有积压订单就需要

额外 760 亿美元融资；⾼盛在其乐观情景下给出 229 亿美元，但前提是客⼾预付合同价值的 20%
—— 若剔除预付款，这⼀数字将跳升⾄ 367 亿美元。
对此， CoreWeave 在财报中给出了⾃⼰的答案： " 我们的增⻓由多元融资来源⽀撑，包括客⼾合
同⽀持的融资、经营性现⾦流，以及未来三个⽉内预计约 90 亿美元的客⼾预付款和押⾦。 "
90 亿美元的客⼾预付款规模可观，⼏乎相当于⽬前资产负债表上全部 100 亿美元递延收⼊。但分
析⼈⼠提⽰，预付款的质量同样重要 —— 来⾃坐拥千亿⾃由现⾦流超⼤型科技公司的预付款，

### Page 6

与来⾃⾃⾝也在举债购买算⼒的 AI 初创公司的预付款，⻛险截然不同，后者不过是同⼀笔钱绕了
⼀条路。
本季度融资⼯具包括： 42 亿美元可转换优先票据超额发⾏、最多 3500 万股 A 类普通股的股票市
价发⾏计划（按当前股价约可融资 18 亿美元），以及 26 亿美元 DDTL 5.5 融资设施。
Explosive Options 的 Bob Lang 直⾔不讳： " 他们没有⾜够的现⾦来偿付所有债务。除⾮未来 17 、
18 个⽉内有⼤订单或⼤笔资⾦涌⼊，否则这将是 CoreWeave ⾛向终结的开始 —— 除⾮有⼈愿意
收购他们。但谁愿意接⼿这些债务呢？ "
富国银⾏分析师 Michael Turrin 持相反⽴场，维持增持评级和 115 美元⽬标价。他认为今年的资
本开⽀已基本有资⾦覆盖，但即便在其乐观情景下， 2027 年资本开⽀也⾼达 660 亿美元，⾄少需
要额外 250 亿美元债务融资。当多头情景都需要在 15% 债券收益率下再借 250 亿美元时，空头逻
辑⼏乎不⾔⾃明。

应付账款下降：供应商终于收到了钱
有⼀个数字朝着正确⽅向移动。此前连续三个季度翻三倍、累计达 36.3 亿美元的应付账款，本季
度下降 7.05 亿美元⾄ 29.3 亿美元。应计负债也下降 7.83 亿美元⾄ 56.4 亿美元，两项合计 " ⽋款 " 缩
减 15 亿美元⾄ 86 亿美元。
这是⾃⼤规模建设启动以来的⾸次下降，或许也解释了为何现⾦资本开⽀如此⾼企 —— 上季度
积⽋的账单终于付清了，当然，⽤的是借来的钱。
积压订单 1325 亿美元：数字亮眼，但细节值得推敲
积压订单 1325 亿美元，单季增加 280 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 2.4 倍，数字颇为亮眼。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

但需要注意的是，公司早在 8 ⽉就已披露 Q3 前⼏周签署了超过 250 亿美元的新承诺。这意味着 Q3
余下两个多⽉内，净新增订单仅约 30 亿美元，与同期滚出的营收⼤致相当。在⼀个以订单增速
为核⼼叙事的公司，选举前⼣的 8 、 9 ⽉相对平静，⾄少值得多问⼀句。
外部压⼒：政策逆⻛未散
在业绩亮眼之外，外部环境的不确定性仍在积聚。美国中期选举结果对 AI 基础设施扩张形成新的
政策障碍 ——45 项数据中⼼相关投票议案中，仅 1 项获得通过。德克萨斯州候任州⻓ Gina
Hinojosa 呼吁⽴即暂停数据中⼼开发， CoreWeave 已致函寻求沟通，但尚未获得回应。⾼盛估
计，各州数据中⼼禁令可能冻结约 45% 的美国计划产能。
公司已将超过 1.3 吉⽡的签约产能布局于美国境外（印度、印度尼西亚和西班⽛）。

在客⼾层⾯，微软曾是 CoreWeave 的最⼤客⼾，但该公司近期表⽰将削减资本开⽀，原因之⼀
是部分 AI 云计算商出现项⽬延误，并已收紧对相关项⽬的监管。
此外， CoreWeave 关键供应商兼股东英伟达的⾸席执⾏官⻩仁勋正⾯临⼀项调查，⻩仁勋此前
公开表态，称 CoreWeave 的财务实⼒ " 每天都在改善 " 。
⾯对外界质疑， Intrator 回应称： " 市场叙事改变了，市场情绪波动了，⼈们开始审视 AI 的建设进
程。⽽我们最新的业绩表明，这家公司正在以正确的⽅式、以应有的速度运转，并赢得了客⼾
的持续选择。 "

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher
International Data
There was no major data out yesterday.
Domestic Data
In Australia, the value of dwelling finance approvals surprised
materially to the downside in Q3, the 15.5% decline marking
the largest quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.

The decline looks to be mostly volume-driven, with the number
of loans falling sharply compared to the average size of loans.
Across segments, investor lending values dropped by nearly a
third (see here).

## Document 426: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.

New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

and differences.

## Document 480: US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026EDITORIAL
US MIDTERMS: THE COST OF LIVING IS NOW A PIVOTAL ISSUE
1 AP-NORC, October 2026.
2 Jared Bernstein, Daniel Posthumus, ‘The way we were: Price-level shocks and consumers’ memories’, SIEPR, May 2026.
3 72% of registered voters rank ending the war with Iran as one of the top priorities for the next Congress, including 60% of Republicans (AP-NORC, October 2026).

4 Oleg Korenok, David Munro, Jiayi Chen, ‘Inflation and attention thresholds’, GLO Discussion Paper Series, No. 1175, Global Labour Organization, 2022.
5 Rajashri Chakrabarti, Thu Pham, Beck Pierce, Maxim L. Pinkovskiy, ‘Tracking the K-shaped economy: who’s driving spending?’, Federal Reserve Bank of New York, Liberty
Street Economics, 1st May 2026.
6 The year 2020 is excluded due to the disruptions caused by lockdowns.
In the United States, growth is holding up, unemployment remains low and consumer spending shows no sign of slowing.

On paper, that should flatter the president’s party ahead of the midterms. Yet, Republicans could lose control of Congress.
The rise in the cost of living, which weighs on households far more than macroeconomic aggregates suggest, could be one
of the reasons. On top of persistent inflation, rekindled by the war in Iran, there is also a slowdown in wage growth and
rising housing costs. The election could hinge on affordability, yet whichever party wins will lack the means to address it.

A change in the majority in Congress will nonetheless have an impact on the country’s economy.
On 3 rd November, Americans will elect a new Congress for the next
two years. With Republicans currently in control of the White House,
the House of Representatives and the Senate, the balance of power in
Washington could shift. Polls suggest that the House, at least, could
swing to the Democrats. Beyond the new balance of power, the vote
will reflect how voters view the way the country is being run. Here, we

focus on the cost of living.
INFLATION: THE INCUMBENTS’ BURDEN
The incumbent party goes into the election with two handicaps.
The first is structural: no matter who holds the White House, the pre -
sident’s party almost always loses seats in midterm elections. The
second stems from the combination of robust but uneven growth
and persistent inflation, which is undermining household confidence.
Pre-election polls1 confirm this assessment: the economy and the cost-

of-living top voters’ worries.
Inflation has overshot the Fed’s target every year since 2021. Prices are
up 28% over five years: 31% for housing (not including mortgage rates,
which have more than doubled), and 29% for food. This shock is felt all
the more keenly as it is the most severe in forty years and follows on
from decades of low inflation2. Furthermore, it hits the lowest-income
households the hardest. In addition, the election comes after an ener-

gy-driven rise in inflation. Energy accounts for the entire gap between
the latest CPI reading (3.7% y/y in September) and the last figure re -
corded before the conflict in the Middle East started (2.4% y/y).
Fuel has made things worse. The rise in the price of a gallon (46%
between February and October) caused by the Iran war — which is
unpopular even amongst Republican voters3 — is an aggravating factor.
In 2026, inflation only slightly crossed the ‘attention threshold’, the

3.6%y/y rate beyond which households start to regard it as problema -
tic4. Fuel is a recurring, unavoidable expense whose price is directly
observable, unlike many items in the CPI basket. This results in a subs-
tantial gap between perceived inflation and measured inflation.
Inflation must be viewed alongside the other drivers of households’
well-being. Today, wages, payrolls and the housing market are moving
the wrong way for most Americans.
IT'S NOT JUST PRICES

The 2022 midterms suggest caution in reading the polls. Democrats
defied expectations of a ‘Republican wave’, retaining control of the Se-
nate and losing the House by just a few seats, even though inflation
averaged 8% that year. This reminds us that inflation must be viewed
alongside the other drivers of households’ well-being. Indeed, how
people experience the cost of living depends on the interplay between
prices, wages and housing. In 2022, wages, payrolls and the housing

market were all growing faster than in 2026. Today, all three are mo -
ving the wrong way for most Americans.
Wage growth is slowing just as inflation picks up again. In September
2026, average hourly earnings were up 3% y/y in nominal terms, the
slowest rate since 2018, and now below inflation. Real wage earnings
are therefore falling (-0.7% y/y in September, with six out of nine mon-
ths showing a decline in 2026). The rise in the cost of living is thus

compounded by a net loss of purchasing power. The Atlanta Fed reports
that wage growth is lower for the bottom quartile of earners (the 25%
of employees with the lowest pay) than for the top quartile (the 25%
with the highest pay): 3.8% y/y vs. 4.4% on average since late 2024,
following 10 years in which the gap favoured the bottom quartile. Top
earners also benefit most from the wealth effect linked to stock mar -
ket valuations. This explains the resilience of aggregate consumption.

However, this rests on a relatively narrow base, mostly households
earning more than USD 125,000/year5. Meanwhile, a falling saving rate
(3.8% in September 2026, down 1.4pp y/y) suggests households are
drawing on their savings to offset price rises. A large share of the po -
pulation is therefore excluded from the growth that has been observed.
Beyond wages, there is the question of employment prospects.
The unemployment rate remains low and consistent with full employ-

ment. Yet the labour market looks stagnant: low firing despite repeated
shocks and low hiring despite solid growth. Payroll growth (an average
of 68,000 per month since January) is well below that of the previous
decade (nearly 200,000 per month between 2011 and 2019 6). Com-
bined with a workforce that is no longer growing due to an ageing
population and a restrictive immigration policy, this is enough to keep
the unemployment rate low and the labour market looking healthy.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
But households are likely to be suffering from a lack of opportunities,
and consequently from a lack of prospects for wage growth that would
help them to increase their purchasing power. The Conference Board’s
labour differential, a gauge of perceived job availability, says as much:
outside the pandemic, it is at its lowest since 2015.
Housing is the area that has deteriorated the most. The rise in

mortgage rates that began in 2022, from under 4% to over 7%, has
dampened the property market. This is true on the demand side, as
expected, but also on the supply side. The sharp rise in interest rates
is deterring homeowners with low-interest mortgages from selling, as
they would have to borrow again at current rates. This ‘lock-in effect’
prevents prices from falling despite weaker demand. The lock-in effect
is also affecting tenants, as the decline in the availability of housing is

driving up rents. Housing lies at the heart of the disconnect between
the macroeconomic picture and voters’ perception of it, as they face a
multifaceted rise in the cost of living. That rise is also driven by credit:
monthly repayments on a home of the same value have risen shar -
ply. This additional cost, which inflation figures do not capture7, erodes
households’ purchasing power in the property market.
A CONGRESS POWERLESS ON THE COST OF LIVING

The next Congress will, in all likelihood, have little room for ma -
noeuvre. Neither party is within reach of the thresholds required to
override the filibuster (which allows 41 out of 100 senators to block
a bill from coming to a vote) and the presidential veto (two-thirds of
both chambers).
Affordability is likely to be a key issue in the election, but the winners
will lack the tools to address it. The Democrats’ agenda relies notably
on the reintroduction of schemes that were abolished or scaled back

under the current administration (Affordable Care Act tax credits, Me-
dicaid). Even if Congress passed them, they would be subject to a pre-
sidential veto. There is no question of direct support for households or
a restrictive fiscal policy.
Whether inflation falls will depend on two factors: how the conflict in
Iran unfolds and the effectiveness of monetary policy. The labour mar-
ket should hold its current balance: robust growth (see our EcoFlash)

is supporting demand, whilst failing to alleviate supply-side pressures,
and the unemployment rate could continue to fall. Household senti -
ment will continue to depend on how prices develop. As for housing,
it is unlikely to improve in the short term. With the 10-year Treasury
yield still at 4.8% at the end of 2027 (BNP Paribas forecast), 30-year
mortgage rates would, at best, remain around 7%.
THE OTHER ECONOMIC STAKES OF THE VOTE
Historically, divided governments have brought legislative stability,

which is viewed favourably by the markets. But polarisation has in -
creased in recent years and is likely to feature prominently in the bud-
get vote and the debt-ceiling debate. The debt limit, in particular, could
be reached as early as Q1 2027, depending on the Treasury’s borrowing
requirements. The current administration, meanwhile, has taken major
decisions (on tariffs, immigration and Iran) without involving Congress,
a practice that is likely to continue.

7 Marijn A. Bolhuis, Judd N. L. Cramer, Karl Oskar Schulz, Lawrence H. Summers, ‘The cost of money is part of the cost of living: new evidence on the consumer sentiment
anomaly’, NBER Working Paper, No. 32163, 2024.
Risks to the deficit remain tilted to the upside. According to our base -
line scenario, it is expected to remain stable at 6.3% of GDP in 2027,
but the post-election balance of power could add to the cost of the war
in Iran and rising debt-service costs. In particular, interests could align

in favour of increased spending should the Democrats gain control of
both houses of Congress. Avoiding, or ending, a shutdown could require
a trade-off involving mutual concessions from the presidency on key,
popular aspects of the Democratic agenda (such as healthcare), and
from the Democrats on defence.
The AI sector is exposed to the vote. The economic stakes are high
given how much the associated investment cycle and the wealth effect
contribute to US growth. However, at this stage, the risk is not legisla-

tive. As with the cost of living, sweeping legislation is unlikely, all the
more so with a president holding a pro-AI stance, and who holds the
right of veto. The risk lies rather in sentiment and further out. A Demo-
cratic sweep could lead to increased congressional activism on regu -
lation, on concentration in the AI industry, and on the knock-on effects
on the cost of living (data centres and electricity) and on jobs. Putting
these issues on the agenda could create a climate conducive to tighter

regulation in the medium term and weigh on the sector’s valuations.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections will see 36 governors’ seats up
for election. These governors have extensive powers (regarding autho-
risations and incentives) when it comes to the establishment of data
centres in their states.
Finally, the midterms will matter for monetary policy. The Senate
confirms, by simple majority, the president’s nominees to the Fed’s
Board of Governors. Consequently, should the Senate come under De -

mocratic control, President Trump would need a consensus pick to re-
place Governor Powell from 2028 — or to fill any other seat that might
become vacant during the next Congress.
*****
Whatever the outcome of the election, disinflation and, more broadly,
relief from the cost of living, depend less on Congress than on the
White House and, to a lesser extent, the Fed. The key factor lies in the
resolution of the conflict in Iran, coupled with an effective monetary

policy to enable a sustainable return to price stability.
Anis Bensaidani
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

强硬派内部博弈，谈判前景存疑

Rezaei 本⼈近⽇在有关会⾯的传⾔升温后，明显加强了强硬表态。他将任何谈判斥为 " 想象中的
会谈 " ，并坚称伊朗 " 不会从国内移⾛哪怕⼀粒浓缩铀 " 。据报道分析，此类表态或有助于他巩固

⾃⾝地位 —— 此前在伊斯兰堡谈判中展现出妥协姿态的伊朗议⻓卡利巴夫，随后即遭边缘化。
部分中东官员还担忧，伊朗伊斯兰⾰命卫队强硬派总司令 Ahmed Vahidi 的影响⼒可能超过⽴场
相对温和的 Rezaei ，从⽽破坏任何与美⽅的谈判进程。多名中东调停⼈也对近期内达成协议持
怀疑态度，理由是⾰命卫队官员传递的信息愈发强硬。
封锁与谈判，华盛顿内部路线之争
在是否重启谈判的问题上，华盛顿内部存在明显的路线分歧。据报道，国务卿 Rubio 和新任⽩宫
幕僚⻓ Stephen Miller 倾向于维持对伊压⼒，等待德⿊兰主动妥协。
Rubio 周⼆表⽰： " ⽬前，我们的经济打压⾏动对伊朗政权造成的压⼒和破坏效果极佳，总统实
际上没有必要开展⼤规模作战⾏动。 " 他同时补充，美军随时准备好在伊朗越过红线时予以回
应。
调停⽅提出了⼀些建⽴信任的过渡措施：美国将航⺟后撤⾄⾜够远的距离以降低⼤规模打击⻛
险，并适度放宽对伊朗港⼝的封锁；作为交换，伊朗将允许 25 艘油轮通过霍尔⽊兹海峡。但这

⼀⽅案⽬前尚未获得任何⼀⽅的正式确认。
特朗普周⼀下午再度发出威胁，称美国正向该地区增派⼀艘航⺟，并表⽰已 " 全副武装、严阵以
待 " 。他同时表⽰： " 伊朗，我希望我们能与他们达成协议。 "
限免

世芯-KY (3661 TT)

維持評等  
買進(Buy) 
與美國大型客戶完成新簽約，未來成長可期  
⚫ 3 Q26營收符合預期，惟毛利率低於公司指 引 
⚫ 4Q26晶片出貨量提升， 2026年毛利率 20%以上  
⚫ Trainium4 世芯為唯一的後段設計服務商  
3Q26營收符合預期，惟毛利率低於公司指引： 世芯 3Q26營收為 2.42
億美元 (YoY+267%，QoQ+239%)，換算台幣為 260.5億元 (YoY+297%，
QoQ+241%)，營收大幅增加主要得益於 Trainium3 晶片進入量產高原
期。毛利為 1.47 億美元 (YoY+139%，QoQ+75%)，換算台幣為 47億
元(YoY+155%，QoQ+77%)，毛利率為 18%，下滑主要因量產營收比
重提高，以及高毛利 NRE 營收認列遞延。營業費用為 0.33 億美元
(YoY+46%，QoQ+4%)，換算台幣為 10.7億元 (YoY+56%，QoQ+5%)。
營業利益為 1.14 億美元 (YoY+194%，QoQ+119%)，換算台幣為 36.3
億元 (YoY+214%，QoQ+120%)，營運槓桿持續提升。 EPS為 37.7元。 
4Q26晶片出貨量提升， 2026年毛利率 20%以上 :3Q26 面臨供應鏈瓶
頸(晶圓、 ABF載板及封裝產能），若無產能限制營收原可再增加 1億
美元。 4Q26 Trainium3 量產出貨規模進一步擴大，與 Trainium 4完成
Tape-out帶動下，預估 4Q26營收將再創單季新高，毛利率預期與 3Q26
相當，維持在接近 20%水準， 2026年毛利率目標維持在 20%以上。 
Trainium4 世芯為唯一的後段設計服務商 :AWS下一代  Trainium4 專
案已進入最終階段，預計於 4Q26  Tape-out，於 4Q27進入小規模試產，
並於 2028 年正式放量， 量產後推動公司進入新的爆發性成長階段，
在該案中世芯為唯一的實體設計服務供應商，且由客戶與世芯直接向
台積電投片，解決次級晶圓市場分流的問題，主導權與產能優勢更加
顯著。預估2027年營收達到 40~50 億美元，將實現三位數 (Triple -digit)
年成長，動能主要由兩大核心專案推動， AWS Trainium3 晶片取得晶
圓廠更多產能分配， 2027 年出貨量將遠高於 2026 年，該專案本身預
計保持三位數成長；與第二大客戶已完成簽約並啟動專案，客戶為美
國大型業者，預計自 2Q27 開始持續貢獻營收，每年可帶來數十億美
元等級的營收規模， 將成為公司第二大營收來源。  
我們根據 2027年預估 EPS  207元*25倍 PER，調整目標價至 5,175元，
維持買進評等。風險因子 : AI ASIC 市場需求不如預期 、供應鏈產能。

與新簽約大客戶合作方式  
與第二大客戶的合作以工程能力切入，世芯提供先進的實體設計 服務，由世芯
與該客戶直接簽約與對接，公司強調此合作並非單一事件， 2Q27啟動的第一個
專案是建立長期合作關係的開端，未來預計將有更多後續專案接續推出，合作
模式具備可持續性與良好的獲利能力，自 2Q27季開始挹注的營收，主要來自晶
片量產收入，而非僅是一次性的 NRE委託設計費。
